However, this comes with the price — super-luminal electromagnetic perturbations. In other words, the model generates large magnetic fields and does not violate any other conditions; it leads to super-luminal perturbations. Thus, our EFT framework suggests that to construct a field theoretical consistent model of inflationary magnetogenesis, we need two necessary conditions— breaking of conformal invariance and causal propagation of perturbations.

In chapter
6
, we proposed a novel mechanism that explains the Fast radio bursts that utilize the strong magnetic field of a compact object. The energy conversion from GWs to electromagnetic waves occurs when GWs pass through the magnetosphere of compact objects (neutron stars or magnetars) due to the Gertsenshtein-Zel
′
dovich effect.
This conversion produces bursts of electromagnetic waves in the GHz range, leading to FRBs. The mechanism requires that the incoming GWs pass through the neutron star with a magnetic field transverse to the direction of propagation of GWs, resulting in FRBs along the line of sight of the observer. The probability of this event is a product of the probability that the GW passes through the NS and the probability that the emitted EM is along the line of sight of the observer. Hence, the probability of seeing such an event is not very high.
We showed that our model explains why the FRB rates are low and why certain FRBs are random. Furthermore, our model predicts that the progenitor of these events is NS in any galaxy. Moreover, importantly, our model predicts that FRBs can also be extragalactic in origin. We have shown that the mechanism presented in chapter
6
can explain three key features of FRBs—peak flux, pulse width, and coherent nature of radiation. We have also discussed the implications of the mechanism in providing the indirect detection of high-frequency gravitational waves.

7.1
Future outlook

As discussed in chapters
3
and
4
, the Riemann coupling to the electromagnetic fields generates the primordial helical magnetic fields and has interesting consequences for understanding the creation of baryon asymmetry in the universe. We saw that the perturbations equation (
4.17
) contains second-order derivatives of
\mathcal{H}
. Since
{\cal H}
, and
{\cal H}^{\prime\prime}
are different for inflation and bounce models
[
177
]
, the helical modes may provide signatures to distinguish the two paradigms. Also, in chapter
4
, we have seen that small-scale modes of the helical fields which exit the horizon near the end of inflation lead to baryogenesis. However, it will be interesting to extend the analysis to Gluons and study the effects on the asymmetry generated in quarks and the baryons. This could be done by considering the coupling the similar mass dimension 6 operator (Riemann coupling) to the non-abelian SU(2) gauge fields. Another interestingly aspect of the small scales modes discussed in chapter
4
would be to see if these modes have sufficiently large power, because they could also provide a mechanism for the formation of primordial black holes in the early Universe.

Modifications of general relativity provide an alternative explanation for cosmological inflation
[
222
]
.
Modified gravity theories have extra degrees of freedom that might have interesting physical consequences in the early Universe. For instance, Stelle gravity
[
223
]
contains massive tensor modes, and these modes carry more energy than the scalar modes in
f(R)
gravity models
[
224
]
. While the analysis in chapter
5
can be extended to
f(R)
gravity models, it is not straightforward to extend to Stelle gravity. Investigating the EFT of inflation and magnetogenesis in these modified gravity models would be interesting to explore. Also, it will be interesting to extend the EFT of magnetogenesis to bouncing models. The EFT action constructed in chapter
5
can also be extended to include the parity-breaking term, leading to the helical magnetic field. In chapter
5
, we have seen that causal propagation must be considered while constructing the inflationary magnetogenesis models; exploring this issue in other magnetogenesis scenarios would be interesting. In chapter
6
, we have explained the properties of the non-repeating FRBs and focussed on the transient incoming GWs. It would be interesting to see if the Gertsenshtein-Zel’dovich mechanism can also explain the repeating nature of FRBs and also if we can incorporate the incoming continuous GWs to explain the FRBs.

Appendix A
Galileon scalar electrodynamics: Details

In this Appendix, we provide more details on the results from chapter
2
.

A.1
Conformal transformation in 4-D curved space-time

Conformal transformation is a local change of scale, which is given by scaling the metric by a smooth non-vanishing function of space-time
\Omega(x^{\mu})

[
93
,
94
]
:

\tilde{g}_{\mu\nu}=\Omega^{2}(x)g_{\mu\nu}

(A.1)

Let us see how this transformation affects the electromagnetic field tensor
F^{\mu\nu}
in arbitrary curved space-time.

F_{\mu\nu}=\nabla_{\mu}A_{\nu}-\nabla_{\nu}A_{\mu}=\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}-\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}A_{\lambda}-\partial_{\nu}A_{\mu}+\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\nu\mu}A_{\lambda}=\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}-\partial_{\nu}A_{\mu}

(A.2)

Therefore,
F_{\mu\nu}
is invariant under the conformal transformation (
A.1
), and is not affected by geometry of the space.

Now we will see how other geometrical quantities which are derived from the metric, change under this transformation. The Christoffel symbol changes as

\tilde{\Gamma}^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}=\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}+C^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}

(A.3)

where
C^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}=\Omega^{-1}\left(\delta^{\lambda}_{\mu}\nabla_{\nu}\Omega+\delta^{\lambda}_{\nu}\nabla_{\mu}\Omega-g_{\mu\nu}g^{\rho\lambda}\nabla_{\rho}\Omega\right)

Putting this value of Christoffel connetion in the Riemann tensor in the conformal frame (Jordan frame) we get,

\tilde{R}^{\lambda}_{\sigma\mu\nu}=R^{\lambda}_{\sigma\mu\nu}+\nabla_{\mu}C^{\lambda}_{\nu\sigma}-\nabla_{\nu}C^{\lambda}_{\mu\sigma}+C^{\lambda}_{\mu\rho}C^{\rho}_{\nu\sigma}-C^{\lambda}_{\nu\rho}C^{\rho}_{\mu\sigma}

(A.4)

Therefore, Riemann tensor changes under the conformal transformaton, contracting with the metric gives the Ricci tensor

\tilde{R}_{\mu\nu}=R_{\mu\nu}-[2\delta^{\alpha}_{\mu}\delta^{\beta}_{\nu}+g_{\mu\nu}g^{\alpha\beta}]\Omega^{-1}(\nabla_{\alpha}\nabla_{\beta}\Omega)+[4\delta^{\alpha}_{\mu}\delta^{\beta}_{\nu}-g_{\mu\nu}g^{\alpha\beta}]\Omega^{-2}(\nabla_{\alpha}\Omega)(\nabla_{\beta}\Omega)

(A.5)

and Ricci curvature scalar as

\tilde{R}=\Omega^{-2}R--6g^{\alpha\beta}\Omega^{-3}(\nabla_{\alpha}\nabla_{\beta}\Omega)

(A.6)

Hence, we can see that Christoffel connections, Riemann tensor, Ricci tensor and Ricci scalar transform according to the relation given above. So, introducing these quantities in the action break the conformal invariance of the action. It is important to note that for higher derivative field theories ( e.g., Galileon fields) the conformal invariance is explicitly broken as

\tilde{\nabla}_{\mu}\phi=\nabla_{\mu}\phi=\partial_{\mu}\phi

(A.7)

\displaystyle\tilde{\nabla}_{\mu}\tilde{\nabla}_{\nu}\phi=\nabla_{\mu}\nabla_{\nu}\phi-(\;\delta^{\alpha}_{\mu}\;\delta^{\beta}_{\nu}\;+\;\delta^{\beta}_{\mu}\;\delta^{\alpha}_{\nu}\;)\;\Omega^{-1}\;(\nabla_{\alpha}\Omega)(\nabla_{\beta}\Omega)

(A.8)

where
\phi
is any real scalar field.

A.2
Fixing the coefficients of
\mathcal{L}_{4}

In this Appendix, we fix all the coefficients in the Lagrangian (
2.1.2
). Demanding that the Lagrangian is invariant under the Galileon transformation (
2.2
) leads to the
following constraint:

\displaystyle 2A_{1}\,\partial_{\mu}\partial^{\mu}\pi^{*}\partial_{\nu}\partial^{\nu}\pi^{*}\partial_{\alpha}\pi a^{\alpha}+A_{2}\,\partial_{\mu}\partial^{\mu}\pi^{*}\partial_{\nu}\partial^{\nu}\pi\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}a^{\alpha}

\displaystyle+B_{1}\,(\partial_{\mu}\partial^{\mu}\pi^{*}\partial^{\nu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi\partial_{\nu}\pi^{*}a_{\alpha}+\partial_{\mu}\partial^{\mu}\pi^{*}\partial^{\nu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi\partial_{\alpha}\pi{a_{\nu}}^{*})+2B_{2}\,\partial_{\mu}\partial^{\mu}\pi^{*}\partial^{\nu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\partial_{\nu}\pi a_{\alpha}

\displaystyle+2C_{1}\,\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\pi^{*}\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi^{*}\partial^{\alpha}\pi a_{\alpha}+C_{2}\,\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\pi^{*}\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}a_{\alpha}

\displaystyle+2D_{1}\,\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi^{*}\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\partial_{\mu}\pi a^{\alpha}+D_{2}\,\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi^{*}\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi\partial_{\mu}\pi^{*}a^{\alpha}+D_{3}\,\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\partial_{\mu}\pi^{*}a^{\alpha}+c.c.=0\,.

(A.9)

Here, c.c. denotes the complex conjugate, we have considered only first order terms in
a^{\mu}
and have ignored the quadratic terms of the infinitesimal shift parameters. All the first order terms in Eq. (
A.2
) either do not contribute or contribute total derivative terms. After integrating by parts (and ignoring the total derivative terms as they do not contribute to the action) and collecting similar terms, we get

\displaystyle\left[(2A_{1}-B_{1})\,(\Box\pi^{*})^{2}\partial_{\alpha}\pi+(A_{2}-2B_{2}-B_{1})\,\Box\pi^{*}\Box\pi\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}-(B_{1}+2C_{1})\,\partial^{\nu}\Box\pi^{*}\partial_{\nu}\pi^{*}\partial_{\alpha}\pi\right.

\displaystyle\left.-(B_{1}+D_{3})\,\partial^{\mu}\Box\pi\partial_{\mu}\pi^{*}\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}+(C_{2}-2D_{1}-D_{3})\,\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\pi^{*}\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-(2D_{1}+2B_{2})\,\partial^{\mu}\Box\pi^{*}\partial_{\mu}\pi\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}-(2C_{1}-D_{2})\,\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\pi^{*}\partial^{\nu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi\partial^{\mu}\pi^{*}\right]a^{\alpha}=0

(A.10)

Only way to satisfy the above equation for an arbitrary
\pi
and
\pi^{*}
is that all the coefficients vanish. This leads to the following relations:

\displaystyle B_{1}

\displaystyle=2A_{1};~C_{1}=-A_{1}

\displaystyle B_{2}

\displaystyle=-A_{1}+\frac{A_{2}}{2}=-E

\displaystyle C_{2}

\displaystyle=-A_{2};~D_{2}=-2A_{1}

\displaystyle D_{1}

\displaystyle=A_{1}-\frac{A_{2}}{2}=E

\displaystyle D_{3}

\displaystyle=-2A_{1}\,.

(A.11)

It is straightforward to see from the above expressions that all
the parameters can be written in terms of two parameters
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
. Substituting the coefficients (
A.2
) in Eq. (
2.1.2
) leads to the following generic complex scalar Lagrangian
\mathcal{L}_{4}
;

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{4}

\displaystyle{}=A_{1}[\,\,(\,\,(\Box\pi)^{2}\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}+2\Box\pi\partial_{\nu}\pi\partial^{\nu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}-\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\pi\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}+\rm{c.c.}\,\,)

\displaystyle{}-2\partial_{\mu}\pi^{*}\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\partial^{\alpha}\pi]+A_{2}[\Box\pi^{*}\Box\pi\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\partial^{\alpha}\pi-\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\pi^{*}\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\partial_{\alpha}\pi]

\displaystyle{}+E[\partial_{\mu}\pi^{*}\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}-\Box\pi\partial_{\nu}\pi^{*}\partial^{\nu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}+\rm{c.c.}]

(A.12)

Now, there are only two free parameters
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
in the Lagrangain.
The action for the above generic Lagrangian (
A.2
) is given by,

{S}_{4}=\int d^{4}x~\mathcal{L}_{4}

(A.13)

We can identify the generic action (
A.13
) as the summation of the following actions:

\begin{split}S^{(1)}_{4}&=A_{1}\int d^{4}x\;(\,\partial_{\mu}\partial^{\mu}\pi\partial_{\nu}\partial^{\nu}\pi\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}+\rm{c.c.}\,\,)\\
{S}^{(2)}_{4}&=A_{2}\int d^{4}x\;\partial_{\mu}\partial^{\mu}\pi^{*}\partial_{\nu}\partial^{\nu}\pi\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\partial^{\alpha}\pi\\
{S}^{(3)}_{4}&=2A_{1}\int d^{4}x\;(\,\partial_{\mu}\partial^{\mu}\pi\partial_{\nu}\pi\partial^{\nu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}+\rm{c.c.}\,\,)\\
{S}^{(4)}_{4}&=-E\int d^{4}x\;(\,\partial_{\mu}\partial^{\mu}\pi\partial_{\nu}\pi^{*}\partial^{\nu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}+\rm{c.c.}\,\,)\\
{S}^{(5)}_{4}&=-A_{1}\int d^{4}x\;(\,\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\pi\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}+\rm{c.c.}\,\,)\\
{S}^{(6)}_{4}&=-A_{2}\int d^{4}x\;\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\pi^{*}\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\partial_{\alpha}\pi\\
{S}^{(7)}_{4}&=E\int d^{4}x\;(\,\partial_{\mu}\pi\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi^{*}\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\partial^{\alpha}\pi+\rm{c.c.}\,\,)\\
{S}^{(8)}_{4}&=-2A_{1}\int d^{4}x\;\partial_{\mu}\pi^{*}\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi^{*}\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi\partial^{\alpha}\pi\\
{S}^{(9)}_{4}&=-2A_{1}\int d^{4}x\;\partial_{\mu}\pi^{*}\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\partial^{\alpha}\pi\end{split}

(A.14)

where

{S}_{4}=\sum_{i=1}^{9}\;{S}^{(i)}_{4}

(A.15)

Having obtained the generic 4th order Galileon action (
A.14
), our next step is to calculate the equations of motion of
\pi
( for
\pi^{*}
is straight forward). We define the quantity
\mathcal{E}_{i}
as the variation of the
{S^{(i)}}_{CGG}
with respect to
\pi
(with respect to
\pi^{*}
for
{\mathcal{E}_{i}}^{*}
) as,

\mathcal{E}^{i}_{4}=\frac{\delta{S}^{(i)}_{4}}{\delta\pi}

(A.16)

Hence, equation of motion for the generic action (
A.15
) is given by,

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{4}=\sum_{i=1}^{9}\;\mathcal{E}_{4}^{i}

(A.17)

Using Eq. (
A.16
), the equations of motion corresponding to nine actions are

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{4}

\displaystyle=2A_{1}\,[\partial_{\mu}\partial^{\mu}\Box\pi\,\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}+4\,\partial_{\mu}\Box\pi\,\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}+2\,\Box\pi\,\partial_{\alpha}\Box\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}+2\,\Box\pi\,\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt-2\,\partial^{\alpha}\Box\pi^{*}\,\Box\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\alpha}\pi-(\Box\pi^{*})^{2}\,\Box\pi]

(A.18)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(2)}_{4}

\displaystyle=A_{2}\,[\partial_{\mu}\partial^{\mu}\Box\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\alpha}\pi\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}+2\,\partial_{\mu}\Box\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}+2\,\partial_{\nu}\Box\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\alpha}\pi\partial^{\nu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}+\Box\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\alpha}\Box\pi\,\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}

\displaystyle{}+\Box\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\alpha}\Box\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\alpha}\pi+2\,\Box\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi\partial^{\nu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}-\partial_{\alpha}\Box\pi^{*}\,\Box\pi\,\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}-\Box\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\alpha}\Box\pi\,\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}-(\Box\pi^{*})^{2}\,\Box\pi]

(A.19)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(3)}_{4}

\displaystyle=2A_{1}\,[\,2\,\partial^{\alpha}\partial^{\nu}\Box\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\alpha}\pi+2\,\partial^{\nu}\Box\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\alpha}\pi\,\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}+2\partial^{\alpha}\Box\pi^{*}\Box\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\alpha}\pi+(\Box\pi^{*})^{2}\,\Box\pi

\displaystyle{}+2\,\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\pi\,\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}+2\,\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\nu}\pi+2\,\partial^{\nu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\pi\,\partial^{\mu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}

\displaystyle{}-\Box\pi\,\partial^{\nu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}]

(A.20)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(4)}_{4}

\displaystyle=-E\,[\,2\,\partial^{\nu}\Box\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi\,\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}+4\,\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi\,\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}+2\,\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi\,\partial_{\mu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}

\displaystyle{}+2\,\partial_{\alpha}\partial_{\nu}\Box\pi\,\partial^{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,+2\,\partial^{\nu}\Box\pi\,\partial_{\alpha}\partial_{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}+2\,\partial^{\nu}\Box\pi\,\Box\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\nu}\pi^{*}+2\,\partial^{\alpha}\Box\pi^{*}\,\Box\pi\,\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}

\displaystyle{}+\Box\pi\,\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\nu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}+(\Box\pi^{*})^{2}\,\Box\pi-\partial_{\nu}\Box\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\nu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\alpha}\pi-2\,\Box\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\alpha}\Box\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\alpha}\pi

\displaystyle{}-2\,\Box\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\nu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi-\partial_{\alpha}\Box\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\nu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\nu}\pi]

(A.21)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(5)}_{4}

\displaystyle=-2A_{1}\,[\,\partial_{\nu}\partial^{\nu}\Box\pi\,\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}+2\,\partial^{\nu}\Box\pi\,\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}+2\,\partial^{\mu}\Box\pi\,\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}

\displaystyle{}+2\,\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi\,\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\mu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}+2\,\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi\,\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,-2\,\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\alpha}\pi

\displaystyle{}-\Box\pi\,\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi^{*}]

(A.22)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(6)}_{4}

\displaystyle=-A_{2}\,[\,\partial_{\nu}\partial^{\nu}\Box\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\alpha}\pi\,\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}+\partial_{\nu}\Box\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\nu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi\,\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}+\partial_{\nu}\Box\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\alpha}\pi\,\partial^{\nu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}+\partial_{\mu}\Box\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi\,\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}

\displaystyle{}+\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi\,\partial^{\nu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}+\partial_{\mu}\Box\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\alpha}\pi\,\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}+\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\nu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi\,\partial^{\mu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}

\displaystyle{}+\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\alpha}\pi\,\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}-\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi\,\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\alpha}\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\pi^{*}-\Box\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi]

(A.23)

\displaystyle\vskip 28.45274pt\mathcal{E}^{(7)}_{4}

\displaystyle=E\,[\,2\,\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\alpha}\Box\pi\,\partial_{\mu}\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}+4\,\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi\,\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\mu}\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}+2\,\partial_{\mu}\Box\pi\,\Box\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\mu}\pi^{*}

\displaystyle{}+2\,\partial_{\alpha}\Box\pi\,\partial_{\mu}\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}+2\,\Box\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi\,\partial^{\nu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}+2\,\partial^{\nu}\Box\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi\,\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}

\displaystyle{}+2\,\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi\,\partial^{\nu}\partial^{\mu}\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\mu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}+2\,\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi\,\partial_{\mu}\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\nu}\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}-2\,\partial^{\nu}\Box\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\alpha}\pi

\displaystyle{}-2\,\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\alpha}\pi-2\,\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\mu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi]

(A.24)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(8)}_{4}

\displaystyle=-2\,A_{1}\,[\,\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\alpha}\Box\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\mu}\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\alpha}\pi+\partial^{\mu}\Box\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\alpha}\partial_{\mu}\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\alpha}\pi+\partial^{\mu}\Box\pi^{*}\,\Box\pi\,\partial_{\mu}\pi^{*}

\displaystyle{}\hskip 28.45274pt+2\,\partial_{\alpha}\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\nu}\partial^{\mu}\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\alpha}\pi+\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\nu}\partial^{\mu}\pi^{*}\,\Box\pi+\partial^{\alpha}\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\mu}\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi

\displaystyle{}\hskip 28.45274pt+\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\nu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi+\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\mu}\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\nu}\Box\pi-\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi\,\partial_{\mu}\pi^{*}

\displaystyle{}\hskip 28.45274pt-\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi^{*}\partial_{\nu}\Box\pi\partial_{\mu}\pi^{*}-\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi^{*}\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi\partial^{\alpha}\partial_{\mu}\pi^{*}]

(A.25)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(9)}_{4}

\displaystyle=-2\,A_{1}\,[\,\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\alpha}\Box\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\mu}\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\alpha}\pi+\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\mu}\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\alpha}\pi+\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\mu}\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\nu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi

\displaystyle{}\hskip 28.45274pt+\partial_{\alpha}\Box\pi^{*}\,\Box\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\alpha}\pi+\partial_{\alpha}\partial_{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\nu}\Box\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\alpha}\pi+\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\Box\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\nu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi

\displaystyle{}\hskip 28.45274pt+\partial_{\alpha}\Box\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\mu}\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi+-\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi\,\partial_{\nu}\Box\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\mu}\pi^{*}]

(A.26)

Thus, the equation of motion for the generic 4th order action (
A.15
) is:

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{4}

\displaystyle=\left(A_{1}+\frac{A_{2}}{2}\right)[\,\,\Box\pi\,\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi^{*}+2\,\Box\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\pi\,\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi^{*}

\displaystyle{}\hskip 28.45274pt-2\,\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\nu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi\,\partial^{\mu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}-(\,\Box\pi^{*})^{2}\,\Box\pi\,]

(A.27)

To compare with the earlier results
[
148
]
, we discuss special cases by taking some specific values of the coefficients
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
.

Case 1:
A_{1}=0

Equations of motion for
\pi
is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{4}^{A_{1}=0}

\displaystyle=\frac{A_{2}}{2}[\,\,\Box\pi\,\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi^{*}+2\,\Box\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\pi\,\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi^{*}-2\,\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\nu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi\,\partial^{\mu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}-(\,\Box\pi^{*})^{2}\,\Box\pi\,]

(A.28)

which in the limit
\pi=\pi^{*}
gives

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{4}^{A_{1}=0}

\displaystyle=\frac{A_{2}}{2}\,[\,3\,\Box\pi\,\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\pi\,\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi-2\,\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\pi\,\partial^{\nu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi\,\partial^{\mu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi-(\Box\pi)^{3}\,\,]

(A.29)

Case 2:
A_{2}=0

Equations of motion for
\pi
is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{4}^{A_{2}=0}

\displaystyle=A_{1}[\,\,\Box\pi\,\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi^{*}+2\,\Box\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\pi\,\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi^{*}-2\,\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\nu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi\,\partial^{\mu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}-(\,\Box\pi^{*})^{2}\,\Box\pi\,]

(A.30)

which in the limit
\pi=\pi^{*}
gives

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{4}^{A_{2}=0}

\displaystyle=A_{1}\,\left[\,3\,\Box\pi\,\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\pi\,\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi-2\,\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\pi\,\partial^{\nu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi\,\partial^{\mu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi-(\Box\pi)^{3}\,\,\right]

(A.31)

Case 3:
A_{1}=A_{2}

Equations of motion for
\pi
is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{4}^{A_{1}\neq A_{2}}

\displaystyle=A_{1}\,\left[\,\frac{3}{2}\,\Box\pi\,\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi^{*}+3\,\Box\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\pi\,\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi^{*}\right.

\displaystyle{}\hskip 28.45274pt\left.-3\,\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\nu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi\,\partial^{\mu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}-\frac{3}{2}\,(\Box\pi^{*})^{2}\,\Box\pi\,\,\right]

(A.32)

which in the limit
\pi=\pi^{*}
gives

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{4}^{A_{1}=A_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{3A_{1}}{2}\,\,[\,3\,\Box\pi\,\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\pi\,\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi-2\,\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\pi\,\partial^{\nu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi\,\partial^{\mu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi-(\Box\pi)^{3}\,\,]

(A.33)

Case 4:
A_{1}\neq A_{2}

Equations of motion for
\pi
is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{4}^{A_{1}\neq A_{2}}

\displaystyle=\left(A_{1}+\frac{A_{2}}{2}\right)[\,\,\Box\pi\,\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi^{*}+2\,\Box\pi^{*}\,\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\pi\,\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi^{*}-2\,\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\partial^{\nu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi\,\partial^{\mu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi^{*}

\displaystyle{}-(\,\Box\pi^{*})^{2}\,\Box\pi\,]

(A.34)

which in the limit
\pi=\pi^{*}
gives

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{4}^{A_{1}\neq A_{2}}

\displaystyle=\left(A_{1}+\frac{A_{2}}{2}\right)[\,3\,\Box\pi\,\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\pi\,\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi-2\,\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\pi\,\partial^{\nu}\partial^{\alpha}\pi\,\partial^{\mu}\partial_{\alpha}\pi-(\Box\pi)^{3}\,\,]

(A.35)

From the above cases, it is interesting to see that equations of motion are independent of the values of
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
and the equations of motion of all the four cases are identical to Ref.
[
148
]
in flat space-time. Hence, for simplicity, we set
A_{1}=0
in the Lagrangian for the fourth order complex scalar Galileon Lagrangian.

A.3
Gauge fixing

In this Appendix, we show that the Galileon complex scalar action (
2.1.3
) is indeed invariant under the Gauge transformation.
Replacing,
\partial_{\mu}\rightarrow{D}_{\mu}=\partial_{\mu}+\alpha A_{\mu}
(where we fix
\alpha
at the end of the calculation) implies

\displaystyle\partial_{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\pi\rightarrow{D}_{\mu}{D}^{\nu}\pi=(\partial_{\mu}+\alpha A_{\mu})(\partial^{\nu}+\alpha A^{\nu})\pi={\partial_{\mu}\,^{\nu}{\pi}+\alpha(\partial_{\mu}{A^{\nu}}\pi+A^{\nu}\partial_{\mu}{\pi}+A_{\mu}\partial^{\nu}{\pi})+\alpha\alpha A_{\mu}A^{\nu}\pi}

(A.36)

Under the local U(1) gauge transformation:

\pi\rightarrow\pi e^{-ie\theta(x)}
, above expression becomes

\displaystyle{D}_{\mu}{D}^{\nu}(\pi e^{-ie\theta})

\displaystyle=e^{-ie\theta}(\partial_{\mu}\,^{\nu}{\pi}+\alpha\partial_{\mu}{A^{\nu}}\pi+A^{\nu}\alpha\partial_{\mu}{\pi}+A_{\mu}\alpha\partial^{\nu}{\pi}+A_{\mu}A^{\nu}\alpha\alpha\pi

\displaystyle{}-ie(\partial^{\nu}{\pi}\partial_{\mu}{\theta}+\partial_{\mu}{\pi}\partial^{\nu}{\theta}+\partial_{\mu}\,^{\nu}{\theta}\pi+A^{\nu}\alpha\partial_{\mu}{\theta}\pi+A_{\mu}\alpha\partial^{\nu}{\theta}\pi)+ieie\partial_{\mu}{\theta}\partial^{\nu}{\theta}\pi

(A.37)

Under the gauge transformation
A_{\mu}\rightarrow A_{\mu}+\partial_{\mu}\theta
, Eq. (
A.3
) becomes,

\displaystyle{D}_{\mu}{D}^{\nu}(\pi e^{-ie\theta})

\displaystyle\rightarrow e^{-ie\theta}[\partial_{\mu}\,^{\nu}{\pi}+\alpha(\partial_{\mu}{A^{\nu}}\pi+\partial_{\mu}{\partial^{\nu}\theta}\pi+A^{\nu}\partial_{\mu}{\pi}+\partial^{\nu}\theta\partial_{\mu}{\pi}+A_{\mu}\partial^{\nu}{\pi}+\partial_{\mu}\theta\partial^{\nu}{\pi}

\displaystyle{}+\alpha A_{\mu}A^{\nu}\pi+\alpha A_{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\theta\pi+\alpha A^{\nu}\partial_{\mu}\theta\pi+\alpha\partial_{\mu}\theta\partial^{\nu}\theta\pi)-ie(\partial^{\nu}{\pi}\partial_{\mu}{\theta}+\partial_{\mu}{\pi}\partial^{\nu}{\theta}+\partial_{\mu}\,^{\nu}{\theta}\pi

\displaystyle{}+\alpha A^{\nu}\partial_{\mu}{\theta}\pi+\alpha\partial^{\nu}\theta\partial_{\mu}{\theta}\pi+\alpha A_{\mu}\partial^{\nu}{\theta}\pi+\alpha\partial_{\mu}\partial^{\nu}{\theta}\pi)+ieie\partial_{\mu}{\theta}\partial^{\nu}{\theta}\pi]

(A.38)

For the
{D}_{\mu}{D}^{\nu}\pi
to be invariant under the local gauge transformations, we set
\alpha=ie
in Eq. (
A.3
). Hence, the action (
2.9
) is invariant under the following
simultaneous transformations:

\displaystyle A_{\mu}\rightarrow A_{\mu}+\partial_{\mu}\theta\,\,;\hskip 14.22636pt\pi\rightarrow\pi e^{-ie\theta(x)}

(A.39)

and the quantity
{D}_{\mu}{D}^{\nu}\pi
will transform as

\displaystyle{D}_{\mu}{D}^{\nu}(\pi e^{-ie\theta})=(\partial_{\mu}\,^{\nu}{\pi}+\alpha\partial_{\mu}{A^{\nu}}\pi+A^{\nu}\alpha\partial_{\mu}{\pi}+A_{\mu}\alpha\partial^{\nu}{\pi}+A_{\mu}A^{\nu}\alpha\alpha\pi)e^{-ie\theta}

(A.40)

Similar analysis can be done for
\pi^{*}\rightarrow\pi^{*}e^{ie\theta(x)}
also.

A.4
Galileon scalar electrodynamics in curved space-time

In this Appendix, we show that the action (
2.2.1
) leads to
higher derivative equations of motion. We also show addition of the
non-minimal term leads to second order equations of motion. The variation of the action (
2.2.1
) with respect to
\pi
yield the EOM:

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{\rm{min}}_{4}

\displaystyle=\frac{\omega}{2\lambda_{\pi}^{6}}[\,\,2\,\nabla_{\mu}\nabla^{\mu}\pi^{*}\,\nabla_{\nu}\nabla_{\alpha}\pi\,\nabla^{\nu}\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}-\nabla_{\mu}\nabla^{\mu}\pi^{*}\,\nabla_{\nu}\nabla^{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\nabla_{\alpha}\nabla^{\alpha}\pi-2\,\nabla_{\mu}\nabla_{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\nabla^{\mu}\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\nabla^{\nu}\nabla_{\alpha}\pi

\displaystyle{}+\nabla_{\mu}\nabla^{\mu}\pi\,\nabla^{\mu}\nabla^{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\nabla_{\mu}\nabla_{\nu}\pi^{*}+2\,\nabla_{\alpha}\pi\,\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,(\,\nabla^{\nu}\nabla_{\nu}\nabla_{\mu}\nabla^{\mu}\pi^{*}-\nabla^{\nu}\nabla^{\mu}\nabla_{\mu}\nabla_{\nu}\pi^{*}\,)

\displaystyle{}+\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\nabla_{\nu}\pi^{*}\,(\,\nabla^{\mu}\nabla_{\mu}\nabla^{\nu}\nabla_{\alpha}\pi+\nabla^{\alpha}\nabla^{\nu}\nabla_{\mu}\nabla^{\mu}\pi\,)-\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\nabla_{\mu}\pi^{*}\,(\,\nabla^{\nu}\nabla^{\mu}\nabla_{\nu}\nabla_{\alpha}\pi+\nabla_{\alpha}\nabla_{\nu}\nabla^{\mu}\nabla^{\nu}\pi\,)

\displaystyle{}+3\,\nabla^{\nu}\nabla^{\alpha}\pi\,\nabla_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,(\,\nabla_{\nu}\nabla_{\mu}\nabla^{\mu}\pi^{*}-\nabla_{\mu}\nabla^{\mu}\nabla_{\nu}\pi^{*}\,)+2\,\nabla^{\nu}\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\nabla_{\alpha}\pi\,(\,\nabla_{\nu}\nabla_{\mu}\nabla^{\mu}\pi^{*}-\nabla_{\mu}\nabla^{\mu}\nabla_{\nu}\pi^{*}\,)

\displaystyle{}+\nabla^{\mu}\nabla_{\mu}\pi\,\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,(\,\nabla_{\nu}\nabla^{\nu}\nabla_{\alpha}\pi^{*}-\nabla_{\alpha}\nabla^{\nu}\nabla_{\nu}\pi^{*}\,)+2\,\nabla^{\mu}\nabla^{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,(\nabla_{\mu}\nabla_{\nu}\nabla_{\alpha}\pi-\nabla_{\nu}\nabla_{\mu}\nabla_{\alpha}\pi\,)

\displaystyle{}+\nabla^{\nu}\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\nabla_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,(\,\nabla_{\nu}\nabla_{\mu}\nabla^{\mu}\pi-\nabla_{\mu}\nabla^{\mu}\nabla_{\nu}\pi\,)+2\,\nabla^{\mu}\nabla^{\nu}\pi\,\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,(\,\nabla_{\alpha}\nabla_{\mu}\nabla_{\nu}\pi^{*}-\nabla_{\nu}\nabla_{\alpha}\nabla_{\mu}\pi^{*}\,)

\displaystyle{}+2\,\nabla^{\mu}\nabla^{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\nabla^{\alpha}\pi\,(\,\nabla_{\mu}\nabla_{\alpha}\nabla_{\nu}\pi^{*}-\nabla_{\nu}\nabla_{\mu}\nabla_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,)\,\,]

(A.41)

Using the following commutation properties of covariant derivatives:

\displaystyle[\nabla_{\mu},\nabla_{\nu}]\nabla^{\alpha}\pi

\displaystyle=R^{\alpha}_{\rho\mu\nu}\,\nabla^{\rho}\pi;\;\nabla_{\nu}\nabla_{\mu}\nabla_{\alpha}\pi-\nabla_{\alpha}\nabla_{\mu}\nabla_{\nu}\pi=R^{\rho}_{\mu\alpha\nu}\nabla_{\rho}\pi;\;

\displaystyle{}\nabla_{\alpha}\nabla_{\nu}\nabla^{\nu}\pi-\nabla_{\nu}\nabla^{\nu}\nabla_{\alpha}\pi=-R_{\nu\alpha}\nabla^{\nu}\pi,

(A.42)

we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{4}^{\rm{min}}=\frac{\omega}{2\lambda_{\pi}^{6}}[\,-2\,\nabla_{\alpha}\pi\,\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\nabla^{\nu}\nabla^{\mu}\pi^{*}\,R_{\mu\nu}-\nabla_{\alpha}\pi\,\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\nabla^{\mu}\pi^{*}\,\nabla_{\mu}R+2\,\nabla^{\mu}\pi^{*}\,\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\nabla^{\nu}\nabla^{\rho}\pi\,R_{\rho\mu\alpha\nu}

\displaystyle-\nabla^{\mu}\pi^{*}\,\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\nabla_{\mu}\nabla^{\nu}\pi\,R_{\nu\alpha}-\nabla^{\mu}\pi^{*}\,\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\nabla^{\rho}\pi\,\nabla_{\rho}R_{\alpha\mu}-3\,\nabla^{\alpha}\nabla^{\nu}\pi\,\nabla_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\nabla^{\mu}\pi^{*}\,R_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle-2\,\nabla^{\mu}\pi^{*}\,\nabla_{\alpha}\pi\,\nabla^{\nu}\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,R_{\mu\nu}+\nabla^{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\nabla^{\mu}\nabla_{\mu}\pi\,R_{\nu\alpha}-2\,\nabla^{\nu}\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\nabla_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\nabla^{\mu}\pi\,R_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle+2\,\nabla_{\mu}\nabla^{\mu}\pi^{*}\,\nabla_{\nu}\nabla_{\alpha}\pi\,\nabla^{\nu}\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}-\nabla_{\mu}\nabla^{\mu}\pi^{*}\,\nabla_{\nu}\nabla^{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\nabla_{\alpha}\nabla^{\alpha}\pi-2\,\nabla_{\mu}\nabla_{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\nabla^{\mu}\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\nabla^{\nu}\nabla_{\alpha}\pi

\displaystyle+\nabla_{\mu}\nabla^{\mu}\pi\,\nabla^{\mu}\nabla^{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\nabla_{\mu}\nabla_{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\,]

(A.43)

Note that the second and fifth terms in the above equation are higher derivative (of metric) terms. In order to remove these terms we need to add some non-minimal terms in the action in such a way that on varying the total action, all the derivatives of the Ricci tensor and Ricci scalar vanish. The equation of motion for the non-minimal action (
2.2.1
) is given by:

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{\rm{nm}}_{4}

\displaystyle=\frac{\omega}{4\lambda_{\pi}^{6}}[\,2\,\nabla_{\mu}\nabla^{\mu}\pi^{*}\,\nabla_{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\nabla^{\nu}\pi\,R+2\,\nabla_{\mu}\nabla_{\nu}\pi\,\nabla^{\mu}\pi^{*}\,\nabla^{\nu}\pi^{*}\,R+2\,\nabla_{\mu}\pi^{*}\,\nabla^{\mu}\pi\,\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\nabla_{\alpha}R

\displaystyle{}-\nabla_{\mu}\nabla^{\mu}\pi\,\nabla_{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\nabla^{\nu}\pi^{*}\,R+2\,\nabla_{\mu}\nabla^{\mu}\pi\,\nabla^{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,R_{\nu\alpha}+4\,\nabla^{\alpha}\nabla^{\mu}\pi^{*}\,\nabla_{\alpha}\pi\,\nabla^{\nu}\pi^{*}\,R_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle{}+2\,\nabla^{\alpha}\pi\,\nabla^{\mu}\pi^{*}\,\nabla^{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\nabla_{\alpha}R_{\mu\nu}+4\,\nabla^{\mu}\nabla_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\nabla^{\nu}\pi R_{\mu\nu}++2\nabla_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\nabla^{\mu}\nabla^{\nu}\pi\,R_{\mu\nu}\,\,]

(A.44)

If we add the above non minimal action in the action (
2.2.1
) then it will cancel all the higher order derivative terms in the EOM. So varying the action

{S}_{4}^{\rm{min}}+{S}^{\rm{nm}}_{4}
with respect to
\pi
, we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\frac{\omega}{2\lambda_{\pi}^{6}}\,\,[\,\,2\,\Box\pi^{*}\,\nabla_{\nu}\nabla_{\alpha}\pi\,\nabla^{\nu}\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}-(\,\Box\pi^{*})^{2}\,\Box\pi+\Box\pi\,\nabla^{\mu}\nabla^{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\nabla_{\mu}\nabla_{\nu}\pi^{*}

\displaystyle{}-2\,\nabla_{\mu}\nabla_{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\nabla^{\mu}\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\nabla^{\nu}\nabla_{\alpha}\pi+\Box\pi^{*}\,\nabla_{\mu}\pi^{*}\,\nabla^{\mu}\pi\,R-\frac{1}{2}\Box\pi\,\nabla_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,R

\displaystyle{}+\nabla_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\nabla_{\mu}\pi^{*}\,\nabla^{\mu}\nabla^{\alpha}\pi\,R+2\,\Box\pi\,\nabla^{\nu}\pi^{*}\,\nabla^{\mu}\pi^{*}\,R_{\nu\mu}+\nabla_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\nabla^{\nu}\nabla^{\mu}\pi\,R_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle{}-2\,\nabla_{\alpha}\pi\,\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\nabla^{\nu}\nabla^{\mu}\pi^{*}\,R_{\mu\nu}-\nabla^{\mu}\pi^{*}\,\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\nabla_{\mu}\nabla^{\nu}\pi\,R_{\nu\alpha}

\displaystyle{}+2\,\nabla^{\mu}\pi^{*}\,\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\nabla^{\nu}\nabla^{\rho}\pi\,R_{\rho\mu\alpha\nu}-3\,\nabla^{\alpha}\nabla^{\nu}\pi\,\nabla_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\nabla^{\mu}\pi^{*}\,R_{\mu\nu}]

(A.45)

A.5
Consistency check with real scalar galileon

In this section, we show systematically that our fourth order action (
S^{\rm{min}}_{4}+S^{\rm{nm}}_{4}
) exactly matches with the results of Deffayet et al
[
149
]
in the
\pi=\pi^{*}
limit. Eq. (
2.18
) can be written as,

\displaystyle\left.{S}_{4}^{\rm{min}}+{S}_{4}^{\rm{nm}}\right|_{\pi=\pi^{*}}

\displaystyle=A_{2}\,\int d^{4}x\,\,\sqrt{-g}\,\,\left[\,(\Box\pi)^{2}\,\nabla_{\alpha}\pi\,\nabla^{\alpha}\pi+\Box\pi\,\nabla_{\nu}\pi\,\nabla^{\nu}\nabla^{\alpha}\pi\,\nabla_{\alpha}\pi\right.

\displaystyle~~-\left.\,\nabla_{\mu}\nabla_{\nu}\pi\,\nabla^{\mu}\nabla^{\nu}\pi\,\nabla_{\alpha}\pi\,\nabla^{\alpha}\pi-\nabla^{\mu}\nabla^{\nu}\pi\,\nabla_{\nu}\nabla_{\alpha}\pi\,\nabla_{\mu}\pi\,\nabla^{\alpha}\pi\,\right]

(A.46)

\displaystyle{}+\frac{A_{2}}{4}\int d^{4}x\,\,\sqrt{-g}\,\,\nabla_{\alpha}\pi\nabla^{\alpha}\pi\nabla^{\mu}\pi\nabla^{\nu}\pi\,\,G_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle{}-\frac{3A_{2}}{4}\int d^{4}x\,\,\sqrt{-g}\,\,\nabla_{\alpha}\pi\nabla^{\alpha}\pi\nabla^{\mu}\pi\nabla^{\nu}\pi R_{\mu\nu}

where
G_{\mu\nu}=R_{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{2}\,g_{\mu\nu}\,R
.
Concentrating on the last term in the above equation (
A.5
) and using the relation
(\nabla_{\mu}\nabla_{\nu}\nabla^{\nu}\pi-\nabla_{\nu}\nabla^{\nu}\nabla_{\mu}\pi)\,\nabla^{\mu}\pi=-R_{\mu\nu}\nabla^{\mu}\pi\nabla^{\nu}\pi
, ignoring the total derivative terms, we get:

\displaystyle-\frac{3A_{2}}{4}\nabla_{\alpha}\pi\nabla^{\alpha}\pi\nabla^{\mu}\pi\nabla^{\nu}\pi R_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=-\frac{3A_{2}}{4}\nabla_{\alpha}\pi\nabla^{\alpha}\pi\nabla_{\mu}\nabla^{\mu}\nabla_{\nu}\pi\nabla^{\nu}\pi+\frac{3A_{2}}{4}\nabla_{\alpha}\pi\nabla^{\alpha}\pi\nabla_{\nu}\nabla^{\mu}\nabla_{\mu}\pi\nabla^{\nu}\pi

\displaystyle{}=\frac{3A_{2}}{2}\nabla_{\mu}\nabla_{\alpha}\pi\nabla^{\mu}\nabla^{\nu}\pi\nabla_{\nu}\pi\nabla^{\alpha}\pi+\frac{3A_{2}}{4}\nabla_{\mu}\nabla_{\nu}\pi\nabla^{\mu}\nabla^{\mu}\pi\nabla^{\nu}\pi\nabla_{\alpha}\pi\nabla^{\alpha}\pi

\displaystyle{}\hskip 28.45274pt-\frac{3A_{2}}{4}(\Box\pi)^{2}\nabla_{\alpha}\pi\nabla^{\alpha}\pi-\frac{3A_{2}}{2}\Box\pi\nabla^{\mu}\nabla^{\nu}\pi\nabla_{\mu}\pi\nabla_{\nu}\pi\,.

(A.47)

Using Eqs. (
A.5
) and (
A.5
), we get:

\displaystyle\left.{S}_{4}^{\rm{min}}+{S}_{4}^{\rm{nm}}\right|_{\pi=\pi^{*}}

\displaystyle=\frac{A_{2}}{4}\,\int d^{4}x\,\,\sqrt{-g}\,\,\left[\,(\Box\pi)^{2}\,\nabla_{\alpha}\pi\,\nabla^{\alpha}\pi-2\Box\pi\,\nabla_{\nu}\pi\,\nabla^{\nu}\nabla^{\alpha}\pi\,\nabla_{\alpha}\pi\right.

\displaystyle{}\hskip 42.67912pt-\left.\,\nabla_{\mu}\nabla_{\nu}\pi\,\nabla^{\mu}\nabla^{\nu}\pi\,\nabla_{\alpha}\pi\,\nabla^{\alpha}\pi+2\nabla^{\mu}\nabla^{\nu}\pi\,\nabla_{\nu}\nabla_{\alpha}\pi\,\nabla_{\mu}\pi\,\nabla^{\alpha}\pi\,\right]

\displaystyle{}+\frac{A_{2}}{4}\int d^{4}x\,\,\sqrt{-g}\,\,\nabla_{\alpha}\pi\nabla^{\alpha}\pi\nabla^{\mu}\pi\nabla^{\nu}\pi\,\,G_{\mu\nu}

(A.48)

Thus, the above action (
A.5
) matches with the action in Ref.
[
149
]
.
It is important to note that there is an overall sign difference between our result and obtained by Deffayet et al
[
149
]
. This is due to the signature convention of the metric and hence our definition of energy-momentum tensor i.e.

T_{\mu\nu}=\frac{2}{\sqrt{-g}}\frac{\delta S}{\delta g^{\mu\nu}}
, differs by an overall negative sing compared to Deffayet et al
[
149
]
.

A.5.1
Galileon scalar electrodynamics in curved space time for
A_{2}=0

Before discussing the other special case
A_{2}=0
, we obtain the non-minimal coupling terms for the full action.
Consider the action corresponding to the full Lagrangian (
A.2
) and varying the action with respect to
\pi
gives the equation of motion for the generic action in curved spacetime. As our main focus is to obtain the non-minimal action, we collect only the fourth order derivative terms in the field as we have done in Appendix (
A.4
).
After collecting the fourth order terms we get,

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{\rm{min}}_{4}

\displaystyle=\frac{A_{2}}{2}\left[\,\,2\nabla_{\alpha}\pi\,\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,(\,\nabla^{\nu}\nabla_{\nu}\nabla_{\mu}\nabla^{\mu}\pi^{*}-\nabla^{\nu}\nabla^{\mu}\nabla_{\mu}\nabla_{\nu}\pi^{*}\,)\right.

\displaystyle{}\left.+\nabla_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\nabla^{\mu}\pi^{*}\,(\,\nabla_{\nu}\nabla^{\nu}\nabla^{\alpha}\nabla_{\mu}\pi+\nabla_{\mu}\nabla^{\alpha}\nabla_{\nu}\nabla^{\nu}\pi-\nabla_{\nu}\nabla^{\alpha}\nabla^{\nu}\nabla_{\mu}\pi-\nabla_{\mu}\nabla_{\nu}\nabla^{\alpha}\nabla^{\nu}\pi\,)\right]

\displaystyle{}+A_{1}\left[\,\,2\nabla_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,(\,\nabla^{\nu}\nabla_{\nu}\nabla_{\mu}\nabla^{\mu}\pi-\nabla^{\nu}\nabla^{\mu}\nabla_{\mu}\nabla_{\nu}\pi\,)\right.

(A.49)

\displaystyle{}\left.+\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\nabla^{\mu}\pi^{*}\,(\,\nabla_{\nu}\nabla^{\alpha}\nabla^{\nu}\nabla_{\mu}\pi+\nabla_{\mu}\nabla_{\nu}\nabla^{\alpha}\nabla^{\nu}\pi-\nabla^{\nu}\nabla_{\nu}\nabla^{\alpha}\nabla_{\mu}\pi-\nabla_{\mu}\nabla^{\alpha}\nabla_{\nu}\nabla^{\nu}\pi\,)\right.

\displaystyle{}\left.+2\nabla_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\,\nabla_{\nu}\pi\,(\,\nabla^{\mu}\nabla_{\mu}\nabla^{\nu}\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}+\nabla^{\nu}\nabla^{\alpha}\nabla^{\mu}\nabla_{\mu}\pi^{*}-\nabla^{\nu}\nabla^{\mu}\nabla^{\alpha}\nabla_{\mu}\pi^{*}-\nabla_{\mu}\nabla_{\alpha}\nabla^{\mu}\nabla_{\nu}\pi^{*}\,)\right]

using the commutation properties of the covariant derivatives and using (
A.4
) we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{\rm{min}}_{4}

\displaystyle=-\frac{A_{2}}{2}\nabla_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\nabla^{\alpha}\pi\nabla^{\mu}\pi^{*}\nabla_{\mu}R-A_{1}\nabla_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\nabla^{\mu}\pi\nabla_{\mu}R-\frac{A_{2}}{2}\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\nabla^{\mu}\pi^{*}\nabla^{\nu}\pi\nabla_{\nu}R_{\alpha\mu}

\displaystyle{}+A_{1}\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\nabla^{\mu}\pi^{*}\nabla^{\nu}\pi\nabla_{\nu}R_{\alpha\mu}-2A_{1}\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\nabla^{\mu}\pi^{*}\nabla^{\nu}\pi\nabla_{\mu}R_{\alpha\nu}\,.

(A.50)

We now consider the following general non-minimal action

\displaystyle S^{\rm{nm}}_{4}=\int d^{4}x\sqrt{-g}\,\,\mathcal{L}^{\rm{nm}}_{4}

(A.51)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\rm{nm}}_{4}

\displaystyle=-\left[\nabla_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\nabla^{\mu}\pi\nabla^{\nu}\pi+\nabla_{\alpha}\pi\nabla^{\alpha}\pi\nabla^{\mu}\pi^{*}\nabla^{\nu}\pi^{*}\right]\left(g_{1}\,R_{\mu\nu}+g_{2}\,g_{\mu\nu}R\right)

\displaystyle{}-\nabla_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\nabla^{\alpha}\pi\nabla^{\mu}\pi^{*}\nabla^{\nu}\pi]\left(h_{1}\,R_{\mu\nu}+h_{2}\,g_{\mu\nu}R\right)

(A.52)

where
g_{1},g_{2},h_{1}
and
h_{2}
are arbitray constants which we will fix later.
Variation of the action (
A.51
) with respect to
\pi
and collecting only derivatives of the Ricci tensor and Ricci scalar terms because we are interested in fixing the coefficients
g_{1},g_{2},h_{1},h_{2}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{\rm{nm}}_{4}

\displaystyle=g_{1}\nabla_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\nabla^{\mu}\pi\nabla_{\mu}R+4g_{2}\nabla_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\nabla^{\mu}\pi\nabla_{\mu}R+\frac{h_{1}}{2}\nabla_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\nabla^{\alpha}\pi\nabla^{\mu}\pi^{*}\nabla_{\mu}R

(A.53)

\displaystyle{}+2h_{2}\nabla_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\nabla^{\alpha}\pi\nabla^{\mu}\pi^{*}\nabla_{\mu}R+2g_{1}\nabla^{\mu}\pi^{*}\nabla^{\nu}\pi^{*}\nabla^{\alpha}\pi\nabla_{\alpha}R_{\mu\nu}+h_{1}\nabla^{\mu}\pi^{*}\nabla^{\nu}\pi\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\nabla_{\alpha}R_{\mu\nu}\,.

Adding Eqs. (
A.5.1
) and (
A.53
) such that all the derivative terms of Ricci tensor and scalar vanish, we obtain the following relations
:

\displaystyle-A_{1}+g_{1}+4g_{2}

\displaystyle=0

(A.54)

\displaystyle-\frac{A_{2}}{2}+A_{1}+2g_{1}

\displaystyle=0

(A.55)

\displaystyle-\frac{A_{2}}{2}+2h_{2}+\frac{h_{1}}{2}

\displaystyle=0

(A.56)

\displaystyle-2A_{1}+h_{1}

\displaystyle=0

(A.57)

which implies

\displaystyle g_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{A_{2}}{4}-\frac{A_{1}}{2}

(A.58)

\displaystyle g_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{3A_{1}}{8}-\frac{A_{2}}{16}

(A.59)

\displaystyle h_{1}

\displaystyle=2A_{1}

(A.60)

\displaystyle h_{2}

\displaystyle=-\frac{A_{1}}{2}+\frac{A_{2}}{4}\,.

(A.61)

Substituting the above values in Eq. (
A.5.1
), we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\rm{nm}}_{4}

\displaystyle=\frac{A_{1}}{2}\left[\nabla_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\nabla^{\mu}\pi\nabla^{\nu}\pi+\nabla_{\alpha}\pi\nabla^{\alpha}\pi\nabla^{\mu}\pi^{*}\nabla^{\nu}\pi^{*}\right]\left(R_{\mu\nu}-\frac{3}{4}\,g_{\mu\nu}R\right)

\displaystyle{}-\frac{A_{2}}{4}\left[\nabla_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\nabla^{\alpha}\pi^{*}\nabla^{\mu}\pi\nabla^{\nu}\pi+\nabla_{\alpha}\pi\nabla^{\alpha}\pi\nabla^{\mu}\pi^{*}\nabla^{\nu}\pi^{*}\right]\left(R_{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{4}\,g_{\mu\nu}R\right)

\displaystyle{}-2A_{1}\nabla_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\nabla^{\alpha}\pi\nabla^{\mu}\pi^{*}\nabla^{\nu}\pi]\left(R_{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{4}\,g_{\mu\nu}R\right)-\frac{A_{2}}{4}\nabla_{\alpha}\pi^{*}\nabla^{\alpha}\pi\nabla^{\mu}\pi^{*}\nabla^{\nu}\pi\,g_{\mu\nu}R

(A.62)

We note that the above non-minimal action identical with
A_{1}=0
case considered in Appendix (
A.4
). In
\pi=\pi^{*}
limit, this case also leads to identical equations of motion as in Ref.
[
149
]
.

A.6
Vector Galileon action
S_{VEG}

In Ref.
[
2017-Nandi.Shankaranarayanan-JCAP
]
, the authors obtained a vector Galileon model that leads to second-order equations.
For completeness, in this appendix, we have listed below the vector Galileon action. The complete vector Galileon action can be written as

{S}_{VEC}={S}_{\rm VG}+\lambda_{\rm VG}\sum_{i=1}^{12}{S}_{Vi}.

(A.63)

where,
\lambda_{VG}
is coupling constant

\displaystyle{S}_{VG}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{\rm VG}\,\int\,d^{4}x\,\sqrt{-g}\,\epsilon^{\alpha\gamma\nu}\epsilon^{\mu\eta\beta}\,\nabla_{\alpha\beta}A_{\gamma}\,\nabla_{\mu\nu}A_{\eta}

(A.64)

\displaystyle{S}_{V1}

\displaystyle=E_{1}\,\int d^{4}x\,\sqrt{-g}\,{g}^{\mu\nu}{g}^{\alpha\beta}{g}^{\gamma\delta}\,R_{\mu\nu}\,{\nabla}_{\alpha}{A}_{\gamma}\,{\nabla}_{\beta}{A}_{\delta}

(A.65)

\displaystyle{S}_{V2}

\displaystyle=E_{2}\,\int d^{4}x\,\sqrt{-g}\,{g}^{\mu\alpha}{g}^{\nu\beta}{g}^{\gamma\delta}\,{R}_{\mu\nu}\,{\nabla}_{\alpha}{A}_{\gamma}\,{\nabla}_{\beta}{A}_{\delta}

(A.66)

\displaystyle{S}_{V3}

\displaystyle=E_{3}\,\int d^{4}x\,\sqrt{-g}\,{g}^{\mu\nu}{g}^{\alpha\beta}{g}^{\gamma\delta}\,{R}_{\mu\nu}\,{\nabla}_{\alpha}{A}_{\beta}\,{\nabla}_{\gamma}{A}_{\delta}

(A.67)

\displaystyle{S}_{V4}

\displaystyle=E_{4}\,\int d^{4}x\,\sqrt{-g}\,{g}^{\mu\nu}{g}^{\alpha\delta}{g}^{\gamma\beta}\,{R}_{\mu\nu}\,{\nabla}_{\alpha}{A}_{\beta}\,{\nabla}_{\gamma}{A}_{\delta}

(A.68)

\displaystyle{S}_{V5}

\displaystyle=E_{5}\,\int d^{4}x\,\sqrt{-g}\,{g}^{\mu\gamma}{g}^{\alpha\beta}{g}^{\nu\delta}\,{R}_{\mu\nu}\,{\nabla}_{\alpha}{A}_{\beta}\,{\nabla}_{\gamma}{A}_{\delta}

(A.69)

\displaystyle{S}_{V6}

\displaystyle=E_{6}\,\int d^{4}x\,\sqrt{-g}\,{g}^{\mu\alpha}{g}^{\nu\delta}{g}^{\gamma\beta}\,{R}_{\mu\nu}\,{\nabla}_{\alpha}{A}_{\beta}\,{\nabla}_{\gamma}{A}_{\delta}

(A.70)

\displaystyle{S}_{V7}

\displaystyle=E_{7}\,\int d^{4}x\,\sqrt{-g}\,{g}^{\mu\alpha}{g}^{\nu\beta}{g}^{\gamma\zeta}{g}^{\delta\eta}\,{R}_{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}\,{\nabla}_{\mu}{A}_{\nu}\,{\nabla}_{\zeta}{A}_{\eta}

(A.71)

\displaystyle{S}_{V8}

\displaystyle=E_{8}\,\int d^{4}x\,\sqrt{-g}\,{g}^{\mu\alpha}{g}^{\eta\beta}{g}^{\gamma\zeta}{g}^{\delta\nu}\,{R}_{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}\,{\nabla}_{\mu}{A}_{\nu}\,{\nabla}_{\zeta}{A}_{\eta}~~~~~~

(A.72)

\displaystyle{S}_{V9}

\displaystyle=E_{9}\,\int d^{4}x\,\sqrt{-g}\,{g}^{\alpha\beta}{g}^{\gamma\delta}{g}^{\mu\nu}\,{R}_{\alpha\beta}\,{R}_{\gamma\delta}\,{A}_{\mu}{A}_{\nu}

(A.73)

\displaystyle{S}_{V10}

\displaystyle=E_{10}\,\int d^{4}x\,\sqrt{-g}\,{g}^{\alpha\beta}{g}^{\gamma\mu}{g}^{\delta\nu}\,{R}_{\alpha\beta}\,{R}_{\gamma\delta}\,{A}_{\mu}{A}_{\nu}

(A.74)

\displaystyle{S}_{V11}

\displaystyle=E_{11}\,\int d^{4}x\,\sqrt{-g}\,{g}^{\alpha\gamma}{g}^{\beta\delta}{g}^{\mu\nu}\,{R}_{\alpha\beta}\,{R}_{\gamma\delta}\,{A}_{\mu}{A}_{\nu}~~~~~~

(A.75)

\displaystyle{S}_{V12}

\displaystyle=E_{12}\,\int d^{4}x\,\sqrt{-g}\,{g}^{\alpha\gamma}{g}^{\beta\mu}{g}^{\delta\nu}\,{R}_{\alpha\beta}\,{R}_{\gamma\delta}\,{A}_{\mu}{A}_{\nu}

(A.76)

where
E_{i}
’s are dimensionless coefficients. Note that
E_{i}
are related to
\lambda_{\rm VG}
.

Appendix B
Helical magnetic field from Riemann coupling: Details

In this Appendix, we provide more details on the results from chapter
3
.

B.1
Series expansion of the Bessel function

For completeness, in this appendix, we obtain the series expansion of the Bessel functions to the leading order terms
[
294
]
. It is useful to define the following quantity:

\displaystyle F(\tau)=\exp\left[\frac{i\pi}{\alpha}\left\lfloor\frac{\pi-\arg(\tau)-\arg\left(\frac{\sqrt{k}\,\varsigma}{\alpha}\right)}{2\pi}\right\rfloor\right]

(B.1)

where
\left\lfloor\cdots\right\rfloor
represents the floor function. Up to leading order, the series expansion for the Bessel functions are:

\displaystyle J_{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\left(\frac{\varsigma\,\sqrt{k}}{\alpha}\tau\,\right)=\frac{F(\tau)}{\Gamma\left(1+\frac{1}{2\alpha}\right)}\left(\frac{\varsigma\,\sqrt{k}}{2\alpha}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\tau^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\left(1-\frac{\varsigma^{2}k\tau^{2}}{2\alpha(1+2\alpha)}+O\left(\tau^{3}\right)\right),

(B.2)

\displaystyle Y_{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\left(\frac{\varsigma\,\sqrt{k}}{\alpha}\tau\,\right)=\frac{F(\tau)}{\pi}\left(\frac{\varsigma\,\sqrt{k}}{2\alpha}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\Gamma\left(-\frac{1}{2\alpha}\right)\cos\left(\frac{\pi}{2\alpha}\right)\left(-\tau^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}+\frac{k\,\varsigma^{2}\,\tau^{2+\frac{1}{2\alpha}}}{2\alpha(1+2\alpha)}+O\left(\tau^{3}\right)\right)

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~+\frac{F(\tau)^{-1}}{\pi}\left(\frac{2\alpha}{\varsigma\,\sqrt{k}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2\alpha}\right)\left(-\tau^{-\frac{1}{2\alpha}}+\frac{k\,\varsigma^{2}\,\tau^{2-\frac{1}{2\alpha}}}{2\alpha(-1+2\alpha)}+O\left(\tau^{3}\right)\right),

(B.3)

\displaystyle J_{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\left(-\frac{i\varsigma\,\sqrt{k}}{\alpha}\tau\,\right)=\frac{\tilde{F}(\tau)}{\Gamma\left(1+\frac{1}{2\alpha}\right)}\left(-\frac{i\,\varsigma\,\sqrt{k}}{2\alpha}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\tau^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\left(1+\frac{\varsigma^{2}k\tau^{2}}{2\alpha(1+2\alpha)}+O\left(\tau^{3}\right)\right)

(B.4)

\displaystyle Y_{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\left(-i\frac{\varsigma\,\sqrt{k}}{\alpha}\tau\,\right)=-\frac{\tilde{F}(\tau)}{\pi}\left(-\frac{i\,\varsigma\,\sqrt{k}}{2\alpha}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\Gamma\left(-\frac{1}{2\alpha}\right)\cos\left(\frac{\pi}{2\alpha}\right)\tau^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\left(1+\frac{k\varsigma^{2}\tau^{2}}{2\alpha(1+2\alpha)}+O\left(\tau^{3}\right)\right)

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~{}-\frac{\tilde{F}(\tau)^{-1}}{\pi}\left(-\frac{2\alpha}{\,i\,\varsigma\,\sqrt{k}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2\alpha}\right)\tau^{-\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\left(1+\frac{k\,\varsigma^{2}\,\tau^{2}}{2\alpha(-1+2\alpha)}+O\left(\tau^{3}\right)\right).

(B.5)

At leading order, the helicity modes are:

\displaystyle A_{+}(\tau,k)

\displaystyle=F(\tau)\,\left(\frac{\varsigma\,\sqrt{k}}{2\alpha}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\left[\frac{C_{1}}{\Gamma\left(1+\frac{1}{2\alpha}\right)}-\frac{C_{2}}{\pi}\Gamma\left(-\frac{1}{2\alpha}\right)\cos\left(\frac{\pi}{2\alpha}\right)\right]

\displaystyle-~~C_{2}\frac{F(\tau)^{-1}}{\pi}\left(\frac{2\alpha}{\varsigma\,\sqrt{k}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2\alpha}\right)\tau^{-\frac{1}{\alpha}}

(B.6)

\displaystyle A_{-}(\tau,k)

\displaystyle=\tilde{F}(\tau)\left(-i\frac{\varsigma\,\sqrt{k}}{2\alpha}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\left[\,\frac{C_{3}}{\Gamma\left(1+\frac{1}{2\alpha}\right)}-\frac{C_{4}}{\pi}\Gamma\left(-\frac{1}{2\alpha}\right)\cos\left(\frac{\pi}{2\alpha}\right)\right]

\displaystyle{}-~~C_{4}\frac{\tilde{F}(\tau)^{-1}}{\pi}\left(-\frac{2\alpha}{i\varsigma\,\sqrt{k}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2\alpha}\right)\tau^{-\frac{1}{\alpha}}

(B.7)

Figure B.1:
Plot of the Floor function for the range of
-10^{8}<\eta<10^{8}
and
-50<\beta<-2
.

It is convenient to define the following quantities:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\tau)

\displaystyle=F(\tau)\,\left(\frac{\varsigma}{2\alpha}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}},

(B.8)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{F}}(\tau)

\displaystyle=\tilde{F}(\tau)\,\left(-i\frac{\varsigma}{2\alpha}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}},

(B.9)

\displaystyle C(\tau)

\displaystyle=F(\tau)\,\left(\frac{\varsigma}{2\alpha}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\left[\frac{C_{1}}{\Gamma\left(1+\frac{1}{2\alpha}\right)}-\frac{C_{2}}{\pi}\Gamma\left(-\frac{1}{2\alpha}\right)\cos\left(\frac{\pi}{2\alpha}\right)\right],

(B.10)

\displaystyle\tilde{C}(\tau)

\displaystyle=\tilde{F}(\tau)\left(-i\frac{\varsigma}{2\alpha}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\left[\,\frac{C_{3}}{\Gamma\left(1+\frac{1}{2\alpha}\right)}-\frac{C_{4}}{\pi}\Gamma\left(-\frac{1}{2\alpha}\right)\cos\left(\frac{\pi}{2\alpha}\right)\right]

(B.11)

B.1
is the 3-D plot of the floor function for the range of values of
\eta
and
\beta
that are consistent with generic models of inflation. As can be seen from the plot, the floor function is zero in the range of interest. Due to this reason, we have suppressed the time dependence in above quantities i.e.,
\mathcal{F}(\tau)=\mathcal{F},\tilde{\mathcal{F}}(\tau)=\tilde{\mathcal{F}}
and
C(\tau)=C,\tilde{C}(\tau)=\tilde{C}
. We have used this in computing the energy densities in Sec. (
3.2
).

B.2
Power spectrum for slow-roll inflation

In Sec. (
3.2
), we obtained the power-spectrum for the helical fields in the power-law inflation. In this section, we obtain the power-spectrum for slow-roll inflation.

To do that, we first obtain the power-spectrum for the power-law inflation by
assuming that
M
is slowly varying and is related to the Hubble parameter

H
, i. e.,
M\sim H\sim\mathcal{H}/{a}
.

B.2.1
Power law inflation

The equation of motion (
4.18
) for power inflation (
4.16
) is:

\displaystyle A_{h}^{\prime\prime}+\left[k^{2}+\frac{8hk\xi}{\eta}\right]\,A_{h}=0.

(B.12)

where
\xi=\frac{\beta(\beta+2)}{(\beta+1)}
, solution of the above equation for super horizon modes are given by

\displaystyle A_{+}(\eta,k)

\displaystyle=2\sqrt{2k\xi\eta}\left[\,D_{1}J_{1}\left(4\sqrt{2k\xi\eta}\right)+2iD_{2}Y_{1}\left(4\sqrt{2k\xi\eta}\,\right)\right]

(B.13)

\displaystyle A_{-}(\eta,k)

\displaystyle=-2\sqrt{2k\xi\eta}\left[\,D_{3}I_{1}\left(4\sqrt{2k\xi\eta}\right)+2D_{4}\,K_{1}\left(4\sqrt{2k\xi\eta}\right)\right]\,,

(B.14)

where
D_{1},D_{2}(D_{3},D_{4})
are the arbitrary constants, we assume these are of the same order of
C_{1},C_{2}(C_{3},C_{4})
respectively. As was shown earlier, we can set
\left|A_{-}(\eta,k)\right|=0
, and positive mode can be approximated by power series at leading order as

\displaystyle A_{+}(\eta,k)

\displaystyle=8D_{1}\xi k\eta+D_{2}\,\frac{2i}{\pi}\,.

(B.15)

where we have used
F(\eta,k)=0
in the above eq. (
B.15
), therefore spectral magnetic energy density at horizon exit is given by

\displaystyle\left.\frac{d\rho_{B}}{d\rm{ln}k}\right|_{k_{*}\eta_{*}\sim 1}=\frac{(-\eta_{0})^{4\beta+4}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{2}}k_{*}^{4\beta+9}\left(\,\,\left|8D_{1}\xi+D_{2}\frac{2i}{\pi}\right|^{2}\,\,\,\right)

(B.16)

Unlike the exact calculation in Sec. (
3.2
), this approximation leads to scale invariant spectrum for
\beta=-\frac{9}{4}
. Substituting the values of the coefficients
D_{1}
and
D_{2}
from eq.(
3.42
), we obtain:

\displaystyle\left.\frac{d\rho_{B}}{d\rm{ln}k}\right|_{k_{*}\eta_{*}\sim 1}\approx 10^{59}\rm{GeV}^{4}=10^{3}\mathcal{H}^{4}

(B.17)

Thus,
R\sim 10^{-9}
. This implies that the above approximation leads to a reduction in the helical field power-spectrum. We will now use this procedure to evaluate the power-spectrum in slow-roll inflation.

B.2.2
Slow roll inflation

The slow-roll parameter
\epsilon
in terms of
\mathcal{H}
is defined by

\displaystyle\epsilon=1-\frac{\mathcal{H}^{\prime}}{\mathcal{H}^{2}}

(B.18)

A necessary condition for inflation is
\epsilon<1
. For the leading order slow-roll, we have:

\mathcal{H}\approx-\frac{(1+\epsilon)}{\eta};~\mathcal{H}^{\prime\prime}\approx-\frac{2(1+\epsilon)}{\eta^{3}};

(B.19)

Substituting these in Eq. (
4.18
), we have:

\displaystyle A_{h}^{\prime\prime}+\left[k^{2}-\frac{16kh}{\eta}\frac{\epsilon}{1+\epsilon}\,\right]\,A_{h}\approx 0.

(B.20)

Note that the above equation (
B.20
) can also be obtained by substituting
\beta=-2-\epsilon
in Eq. (
B.12
) where the expression of
\xi
will be
\xi=-\frac{2\epsilon}{1+\epsilon}
. Hence, the super-horizon mode solution can be obtained by substituting
\xi=-\frac{2\epsilon}{1+\epsilon}
in eq.(
B.13
) and (
B.14
). Thus after setting
\left|A_{-}(\eta,k)\right|=0
, spectral magnetic energy density for slow roll case will have the form

\displaystyle\left.\frac{d\rho_{B}}{d\rm{ln}k}\right|_{k_{*}\eta_{*}\sim 1}

\displaystyle={\frac{1}{\left(2\pi\right)^{2}}\frac{k_{*}^{1-4\epsilon}}{(-\eta_{0})^{4\epsilon+4}}\left(\,\,\left|-\frac{16\epsilon}{1+\epsilon}D_{1}+\frac{2i}{\pi}D_{2}\right|^{2}\,\,\,\right)}

(B.21)

Unlike the exact calculation in Sec. (
3.2
), this approximation leads to blue-tilt spectrum. Thus, this is not a approximation to obtain power-spectrum for helical magnetic fields.

Appendix C
Helical magnetic field from Riemann coupling lead to baryogenesis: Details

In this Appendix, we provide more details on the results from chapter
4
.

C.1
Quantization in the Helicity basis

In this section, we briefly discuss the evolution of the quantum fluctuations of the electromagnetic field in the helicity basis
[
105
]
. Decomposition of the vector potential in Fourier domain leads to:

\displaystyle A^{i}(\vec{x},\eta)=\int\frac{d^{3}k}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{\lambda=1,2}\varepsilon^{i}_{\lambda}\left[A_{\lambda}(k,\eta)b_{\lambda}(\vec{k})e^{ik\cdot x}+A^{*}_{\lambda}(k,\eta)b^{\dagger}_{\lambda}(\vec{k})e^{-ik\cdot x}\right]

(C.1)

where
b(\textbf{k})
and
b^{\dagger}(\textbf{k})
are the annihilation and creation operators respectively for a given comoving mode
k
, and
\varepsilon_{\lambda}^{i}
is the orthogonal basis vector which in right-handed coordinate system
[
105
]
is given by

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\mu}=\left(\frac{1}{a},\textbf{0}\right),\,\,\,\,\varepsilon^{\mu}=\left(0,\frac{\hat{\varepsilon}^{i}_{\lambda}}{a}\right),\,\,\,\,\varepsilon^{\mu}_{3}=\left(0,\frac{\hat{\textbf{k}}}{a}\right)\quad\text{for}\quad\lambda=1,2\,,

(C.2)

3-vectors
\hat{\varepsilon}^{i}_{\lambda}
are unit vectors orthogonal to
\hat{\textbf{k}}
and to each other. Substituting Eq. (
C.2
) in
Eq. (
C.1
) and defining the new variable

\bar{A}_{\lambda}=a(\eta)\,A_{\lambda}(k,\eta)
, we have:

\displaystyle A_{i}(\textbf{x},\eta)=\int\frac{d^{3}k}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{\lambda=1,2}\,\hat{\varepsilon}_{i\lambda}\left[\bar{A}_{\lambda}b_{\lambda}(\textbf{k})e^{i\textbf{k}\cdot\textbf{x}}+\bar{A}^{*}_{\lambda}b^{\dagger}_{\lambda}(\vec{k})e^{-i\textbf{k}\cdot\textbf{x}}\right]\,.

(C.3)

Substituting Eq. (
C.3
) in Eq. (
4.17
), we get:

\displaystyle\sum_{\lambda=1,2}b_{\lambda}\left[\hat{\varepsilon}_{i\lambda}\bar{A}_{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}+\frac{4i}{M^{2}}\epsilon_{ijl}k_{j}\hat{\varepsilon}_{l\,\lambda}\bar{A}_{\lambda}\,\left(\frac{a^{\prime\prime\prime}}{a^{3}}-3\frac{a^{\prime\prime}a^{\prime}}{a^{4}}\right)+k^{2}\hat{\varepsilon}_{i\lambda}\bar{A}_{\lambda}\right]=0

(C.4)

where we have used
\partial_{j}\partial_{j}=-k^{2}
.

Since the action (
4.11
) contains parity breaking term (helicity term), it is useful to work in the helicity basis. The helicity basis vectors
\varepsilon_{+}
and
\varepsilon_{-}
corresponding to
h=+1
and
h=-1
are defined as

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\pm}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\hat{\varepsilon}_{1}\pm i\hat{\varepsilon}_{2}\right).

(C.5)

Assuming that the wave propagates in the
z-
direction, the vector potential in the helicity basis is given by:

\displaystyle\bar{\textbf{A}}=\bar{A}_{1}\hat{\varepsilon}_{1}+\bar{A}_{2}\hat{\varepsilon}_{2}=A_{+}\varepsilon_{+}+A_{-}\varepsilon_{-}

(C.6)

where
A_{+}
(
A_{-}
) refer to the vector potential with positive (negative) helicity.
The ground state in the helicity basis is defined as

\displaystyle b_{h}(\textbf{k})|0\rangle=0

(C.7)

and satisfy the following commutation relations:

\displaystyle\left[b_{h}(\textbf{k}),b^{\dagger}_{h^{\prime}}(\textbf{q})\right]

\displaystyle=\left(2\pi\right)^{3}\,\delta^{3}(\textbf{k}-\textbf{q})\,\delta_{hh^{\prime}}

(C.8)

\displaystyle\left[b_{h}(\textbf{k}),b_{h^{\prime}}(\textbf{q})\right]

\displaystyle=0=\left[b^{\dagger}_{h}(\textbf{k}),b^{\dagger}_{h^{\prime}}(\textbf{q})\right]\,.

(C.9)

Rewriting (
C.4
) in the Helicity basis and replacing

\epsilon_{ijl}\partial_{j}A_{l}\longrightarrow-k\sum_{h=\pm 1}hA_{h}\varepsilon_{h}
, we have:

\displaystyle A_{h}^{\prime\prime}+\left[k^{2}-\frac{4kh}{M^{2}}\,\Gamma(\eta)\right]A_{h}=0\,,

(C.10)

where,

\Gamma(\eta)=\frac{a^{\prime\prime\prime}}{a^{3}}-3\frac{a^{\prime\prime}a^{\prime}}{a^{4}}=\frac{1}{a^{2}}\left(\mathcal{H}^{\prime\prime}-2\mathcal{H}^{3}\right)\,.

(C.11)

C.2
Generation and evolution of helical modes

Substituting the power-law inflation scale factor (
4.16
) in
Eq. (
4.18
), we have:

\displaystyle{A_{h}^{\prime\prime}+\left[k^{2}-\frac{8kh}{M^{2}}\frac{\beta(\beta+1)(\beta+2)}{\eta_{0}^{3}}\left(\frac{-\eta_{0}}{\eta}\right)^{(2\beta+5)}\right]\,A_{h}=0}\,.

(C.12)

Helicity term vanishes for de-sitter case (
\beta=-2
), which is consistent with the fact that the de Sitter symmetry will not be preserved in the presence of helicity terms. However, it will be non-zero for the approximately de Sitter universe i.e.,
\beta=-2-\epsilon
.
In sub horizon limit (
\left|-k\eta\right|\gg 1
), Eq. (
C.12
) simplifies to:

\displaystyle A_{h}^{\prime\prime}+k^{2}A_{h}\approx 0

(C.13)

and assuming that the quantum field is in the vacuum state at asymptotic past
(Bunch-Davies vacuum state), we have:

\displaystyle A_{h}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}e^{-ik\eta}.

(C.14)

On super-Horizon scales (
\left|-k\eta\right|<<1
), Eq. (
C.12
) becomes:

\displaystyle{\alpha^{2}\frac{d^{2}A_{h}}{d\tau^{2}}+\frac{\alpha(\alpha+1)}{\tau}\frac{dA_{h}}{d\tau}+h\,k\,\varsigma^{2}A_{h}=0}

(C.15)

where

{\varsigma^{2}\equiv-\frac{1}{M^{2}\,\eta_{0}}(2\alpha-3)(2\alpha-1)(2\alpha+1)\,,\tau=\left(-\frac{\eta_{0}}{\eta}\right)^{\alpha}\,,\alpha=\beta+\frac{3}{2}}

(C.16)

Note that
\tau
(dimensionless variable
0<\tau<\infty
) and
\eta
(negative during inflation) are linearly related. [At the start of inflation,
\tau
is large and vanishes
at the end of inflation.] Note that
\alpha=-\frac{1}{2}
corresponds to de-sitter and
\alpha\leq-\frac{1}{2}
.
The solutions for the above equation (
C.15
) are:

\displaystyle A_{+}(\tau,k)

\displaystyle=\tau^{-\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\,J_{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\left(\frac{\varsigma\sqrt{k}}{\alpha}\tau\,\right)C_{1}+\tau^{-\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\,Y_{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\left(\frac{\varsigma\sqrt{k}}{\alpha}\tau\right)C_{2}

(C.17a)

\displaystyle A_{-}(\tau,k)

\displaystyle=\tau^{-\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\,J_{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\left(-i\frac{\varsigma\,\sqrt{k}}{\alpha}\tau\right)C_{3}+\tau^{-\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\,Y_{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\left(-i\,\frac{\varsigma\,\sqrt{k}}{\alpha}\tau\right)C_{4}\,,

(C.17b)

where
C_{1},C_{2},C_{3},C_{4}
are arbitrary constants of dimension
L^{1/2}
.
For the two helicity modes, we fix the constants
C_{1},C_{2}
(
C_{3},C_{4})
by matching
A_{h}
and
A_{h}^{\prime}
at the transition time of sub-horizon and super-horizon modes at
k_{*}\sim\eta_{*}^{-1}
where
*
refers to the
quantities evaluated at the horizon-exit.

Although the analysis can be done for any general value of
\alpha
, to keep the calculations tractable, we obtain the constants for
\alpha=-1
. There are two reasons for this choice: First, in this special case,
\tau\propto\eta
and the super-horizon modes can be written in terms of
\eta
using the linear relation.
Second, the constants
C_{1},C_{2},C_{3},C_{4}
have a weak dependence of
\alpha

and, hence, finding the value for a given value of
\alpha
will be accurate within an order
[
120
]
. Thus, matching the solutions and the derivatives at the horizon-exit, we get:

\displaystyle C_{1}

\displaystyle=-e^{i}\,\sqrt{\frac{\pi\eta_{0}}{2}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Theta}}\rm{sin}\Theta+i\sqrt{\Theta}\,\rm{cos}\Theta\right),\,\,\,\,C_{2}=-i\,e^{i}\,\sqrt{\frac{\pi\eta_{0}}{2}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Theta}}\rm{cos}\Theta-i\sqrt{\Theta}\,\rm{sin}\Theta\right)

(C.18)

\displaystyle C_{3}

\displaystyle=e^{i}\,\sqrt{\frac{\pi\eta_{0}}{2}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{i\Theta}}\rm{sinh}\Theta+\sqrt{i\Theta}\,\rm{cosh}\Theta\right),\,\,\,\,C_{4}=-i\,e^{i}\,\sqrt{\frac{\pi\eta_{0}}{2}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{i\Theta}}\rm{cosh}\Theta+\sqrt{i\Theta}\,\rm{sinh}\Theta\right).

where
\Theta=\sqrt{\frac{15\eta_{*}}{M^{2}\eta_{0}^{3}}}
is the dimensionless constant.

In Ref.
[
120
]
,
the current authors derived the magnetic field spectral energy density and is given by

\displaystyle\frac{d\rho_{B}}{d\rm{ln}k}=\left|{\cal C}(k_{*},\alpha)\right|^{2}\,\left(\frac{k}{k_{*}}\right)^{2-4\alpha}k^{3+4\alpha+\frac{1}{2\alpha}}+\left|{\cal C}_{2}(k_{*},\alpha)\right|^{2}\,\left(\frac{k}{k_{*}}\right)^{4-4\alpha}k^{1+4\alpha-\frac{1}{2\alpha}}

(C.19)

Figure C.1:
Plot showing the strength of the present day primordial helical magnetic field at different length scales (from Mpc to Gpc), for two different pivot scales.

where
k_{*}
is the pivot scale, and

{\cal C}(k_{*},\alpha)
and
{\cal C}_{2}(k_{*},\alpha)
are constants that depend on the inflationary energy scale (See Eq. 44 in Ref.
[
120
]
). For de Sitter inflation (
\alpha=-1/2
),
the present day magnetic field as a function of
k
is given by:

B_{0}(k)\sim 10^{-20}\left(\frac{k}{k_{*}}\right)^{2}\,\rm{G}

(C.20)

where we have included only the leading order contribution and have discarded the subleading contribution.

In
C.1
we have plotted the power spectrum of the present day primordial helical magnetic field at different length scales for two pivot scales
k_{*}=0.002\,Mpc^{-1}

[
25
]
and
k_{*}=0.05\,Mpc^{-1}

[
183
]
. One can see from figure
C.1
(left plot) that for around Mpc scale, the value of present day magnetic field is
10^{-15}G
. This is consistent with the current observations
[
192
,
80
,
81
,
78
,
87
]
.

Using the fact that modes exit the horizon around 5 e-foldings,

\displaystyle\eta_{*}=\eta_{end}\cdot 10^{2}

(C.21)

and
\mathcal{H}\sim{\eta_{0}}^{-1}\sim 10^{14}\rm{GeV}
, for
M\sim 10^{14}-10^{17}\rm{GeV}

[
120
,
176
]
, we obtain

\Theta\approx\sqrt{\frac{\eta_{end}\cdot 10^{45}GeV^{3}}{M^{2}}}\,,

which is very small value.
Note also that

\eta_{end}=-\frac{1}{a(\eta_{end})H}=-\frac{e^{-N_{\rm Inf}}}{H_{\rm Inf}}\approx 10^{-41}\,{\rm GeV}^{-1}\,.

Using the fact that
\Theta
is very small, we get

\displaystyle|C_{1}|\approx|C_{3}|\approx\sqrt{\Theta\,\eta_{0}}\,\,,\qquad\text{and}\qquad|C_{2}|\approx|C_{4}|\approx\sqrt{\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Theta}}.

(C.22)

Hence, we obtain the following relations among the coefficients
|C_{1}|\approx|C_{3}|<<|C_{2}|\approx|C_{4}|
.

C.3
BAU parameter for arbitrary values of
\Lambda
and
\mu

Figure C.2:
Plot showing the behaviour of reheating temperature
T_{RH}
(vertical axis) with parameter
M
(horizontal axis), for different ranges of
\Lambda,\mu
. In upper panel ( and lower panel)
\Lambda
is fixed at
10^{14}GeV
( and
10^{12}GeV
) and lower energy scale is varied.

Following Ref.
[
81
]
, we first evaluate the contribution of dilution factor for arbitrary values of
\Lambda
and
\mu
. Assuming instantaneous reheating, the universe transited to radiation domination after inflation. Using entropy conservation i.e.
ga^{3}T^{3}=\mbox{constant}
during its evolution, where
g
is effective relativistic degrees of freedom, we have:

\frac{a_{\mu}}{a_{\Lambda}}=\left(\frac{g_{\Lambda}}{g_{\mu}}\right)^{1/3}\frac{T_{\Lambda}}{T_{\mu}}

(C.23)

where
a_{\Lambda},a_{\mu}
are the scale factors at which the helical modes with energy
\Lambda
and
\mu
reentered the radiation dominated Universe,
g_{\Lambda},g_{\mu}
are the effective relativistic degrees of freedom at which the helical modes with energy
\Lambda
and
\mu
reentered the radiation dominated Universe, and
T_{\Lambda},T_{\mu}
are the Universe temperatures at which the helical modes with energy
\Lambda
and
\mu
reentered the radiation dominated Universe. The Friedmann equation is:

\displaystyle H^{2}=\frac{1}{3M_{\rm P}^{2}}\left[g_{\Lambda}\left(\frac{\pi^{2}}{30}\right)T_{\Lambda}^{4}\right]\,.

(C.24)

The baryogenesis occurs as soon as the helical modes reenter the Hubble radius during the radiation-dominated epoch. For simplicity, we assume that baryogenesis occurs at the start of radiation-dominated epoch, hence we take energy scale of
\Lambda
to be of order
H
. Substituting
T_{\Lambda}
from Eq. (
C.24
) in Eq. (
C.23
), we get,

\displaystyle\frac{a_{\mu}}{a_{\Lambda}}=\frac{g_{\Lambda}^{1/12}}{g_{\mu}^{1/3}}\left(\frac{90}{\pi^{2}}\right)^{1/4}\frac{M_{\rm P}^{1/2}\Lambda^{1/2}}{T_{\mu}}\approx\frac{10^{9}\sqrt{\Lambda}}{\mu},

(C.25)

where
g_{\Lambda}\sim 100
(during reheating) and
g_{\mu}
is of the order 10. Physically, we see that the Universe has expanded by a factor of
\frac{10^{9}\,\sqrt{\Lambda}}{\mu}
when the helical modes of energy
\Lambda
and
\mu
reenter the radiation dominated epoch and hence,
in Eq. (
4.23
), the inverse of this factor will act as the dilution factor.

Setting
\Lambda=10^{14}
GeV, and

\mu=10^{10}
GeV, in the above expression, we have:

\frac{a_{\mu}}{a_{\Lambda}}=\frac{g_{f}^{1/12}}{g_{1}^{1/3}}\left(\frac{90}{\pi^{2}}\right)^{1/4}\frac{\left(M_{\rm P}\Lambda\right)^{1/2}}{\mu}\approx 10^{6}\,.

For the modes that exit the horizon during inflation at
N=5
, we have:

\frac{1}{a^{3}}\sim 10^{-33}\frac{\mu^{3}}{\Lambda^{3/2}}.

Therefore, for generic scales of baryogenesis, the BAU parameter (
4.26
) is given by

\displaystyle\eta_{B}\approx\frac{10^{-38}\cdot\eta_{0}^{2}}{\sqrt{\eta_{end}\cdot 10^{45}GeV^{3}}}\frac{M^{3}\left(\Lambda^{3}\mu^{3}-\mu^{6}\right)}{\Lambda^{3/2}\,T^{3}_{\rm{RH}}}\quad\approx\quad 10^{-11}\left(\frac{M}{M_{P}}\right)^{3}\frac{\left(\Lambda^{3}\mu^{3}-\mu^{6}\right)}{\Lambda^{3/2}\,T^{3}_{\rm{RH}}}.

(C.26)

Using the parametrization in Eq. (
4.28
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{m^{3}\left(\delta^{3}\Delta^{3}\,10^{12}-\Delta^{6}\right)}{\delta^{3/2}\gamma^{3}}\approx n\,10^{22}

(C.27)

where
\mu=\Delta 10^{8}
GeV,
\Delta\in\{1,100\}
and
\gamma\in\{10^{-2},1000\}
.
C.2
shows the behaviour of the reheating temperature as a function of
M
, for different ranges of
\Lambda,\mu
. From the plots we infer that the results obtained in Sec. (
4.3
) by neglecting
\mu
are consistent with the results
in this Appendix.

C.4
Effect of the Riemann coupling near Schwarzschild black hole

In this appendix, we will show that
S_{\rm CB}
coupling is tiny near the
solar mass Schwarzschild black-holes. Specifically, we evaluate at the
Schwarzschild radius of a non-rotating spherically symmetric black hole of mass
\mu
. Since the calculation is order of magnitude, we calculate the Kretschmann scalar
K=R_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}R^{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}
for the Schwarzschild black-hole. For this case, the Kretschmann scalar
K
at a radial distance
r
from the black-hole center is given by:

\displaystyle K=\frac{48G^{2}\mu^{2}}{c^{4}r^{6}}

(C.28)

which implies that the Riemann tensor
\sim\sqrt{K}
. The coupling term

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{K}}{M^{2}}\sim\frac{\sqrt{48}G\mu}{c^{2}r^{3}}\frac{1}{M^{2}}.

(C.29)

For Schwarzschild radius
r_{h}={2G\mu}/{c^{2}}
, the coupling term becomes

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{K}}{M^{2}}\sim\sqrt{\frac{3}{4}}\frac{c^{4}}{G^{2}\,\mu^{2}\,M^{2}}\,.

(C.30)

We set
\mu=1M_{\odot}
where
M_{\odot}\approx 10^{30}\rm{Kg}
is the solar mass. Since the result might be interesting to various astrophysical and cosmological phenomenon, we will calculate the value of the coupling
\sqrt{K}/M^{2}
(
C.30
) in units which are preferred in early universe cosmology (natural units) and gravity (geometrized units).

Natural units are preferred in the early Universe Physics, and all scales are rewritten in terms of
GeV
. For simplicity, we write the following quantities in terms of
m_{P}
as,

\displaystyle G\approx M_{P}^{-2},\qquad c=1,\qquad M\approx 10^{-2}M_{P},\qquad\mu=10^{30}\rm{Kg}\approx 10^{38}M_{P}

(C.31)

where we have used
M_{P}\approx 10^{19}\rm{GeV}\approx 10^{-8}\rm{Kg}
and
M=10^{17}\rm{GeV}\approx 10^{-2}M_{P}
. Using Eq.(
C.31
) in Eq.(
C.30
), we get

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{K}}{M^{2}}\approx 10^{-72}.

(C.32)

To understand the effect in astrophysical phenomenon, we calculate the value of the coupling (
C.30
) in geometrized units (
c=G=1
). Since our model parameter
M\approx 10^{17}\rm{GeV}
has unit of energy, we need to substitute the conversion
M\rightarrow\frac{MG}{c^{4}}
in
Eq. (
C.30
), which gives

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{K}}{M^{2}}\approx\sqrt{\frac{3}{4}}\frac{c^{12}}{G^{4}\,\mu^{2}\,M^{2}}.

(C.33)

Using the following values,

\displaystyle c=G=1,\qquad M=10^{-10}\rm{Kg},\qquad\mu\approx 10^{30}\rm{Kg}

(C.34)

we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{K}}{M^{2}}\approx 10^{-40}\,\rm{Kg}^{-4}.

(C.35)

Hence, from the above analysis, it is clear that the effect near the solar mass size black-hole is negligible. Note that since Riemann tensor is coupled with gauge kinetic terms
F_{\mu\nu}F^{\mu\nu}
, the coupling term contains the frequency of the electromagnetic waves, i.e.,
\omega^{2}
, hence the coupling will have effects at very high frequency.
The coupling constant (
C.30
)
is of the order of one or greater for black-holes of mass
\mu\sim 100M_{P}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{K}}{M^{2}}\sim 1,\qquad\text{for}\quad\mu\sim 100M_{P}

(C.36)

Such size primordial black-holes form in the very early Universe. For such black-holes, Hawking temperature is
[
295
]

\displaystyle T_{H}=\left(\frac{\hbar c^{3}}{Gk_{B}}\right)\frac{1}{8\pi\mu}\quad\approx\quad 10^{-3}M_{P}

(C.37)

Since the Hawking radiation is thermal, we can obtain the peak wavelength of the black-body spectrum from Wien’s displacement law
\lambda_{\rm{max}}T=\rm{constant}
. The wavelength
\lambda_{\rm{max}}
corresponding to
100M_{P}
mass black holes is given by:

\displaystyle\lambda_{\rm{max}}\sim T_{H}^{-1}\approx 10^{3}M_{P}^{-1}

(C.38)

Using the conversion
1~\rm{GeV}=5.06\times 10^{15}m^{-1}
which gives
M_{P}\approx 10^{34}m^{-1}
, we get

\displaystyle\lambda_{\rm{max}}\approx 10^{3}M_{P}^{-1}\approx 10^{-31}m,\qquad\implies\nu=\frac{c}{\lambda_{\rm{max}}}=10^{39}s^{-1}.

(C.39)

Assuming these PBHs are produced just after bigbang, the above frequency will be redshifted by a factor
10^{20}
. Thus, the redshifted frequency is
\sim 10^{19}Hz
(Gamma-ray) and can have potential signatures. However, it is important to note that at that scale, we need to include higher-order corrections.
As mentioned earlier, in this analysis, we have ignored the mass dimension 8 operators
[
161
]
. At the Planck scale, we need to include dimension 8 and beyond. Hence, to understand these effects of PBH of Planck mass size, we need to include mass-dimension 8 operators and beyond.

Appendix D
Effective field theory of magnetogenesis: Details

In this Appendix, we provide more details on the results from chapter
5
.

D.1
Decoupling of vector perturbations and gauge fields

As mentioned in the Introduction, we aim to construct an EFT of magnetogenesis. Like any other effective theory, the EFT of magnetogenesis includes two components: Symmetries and degrees of freedom
[
209
,
210
]
. Thus, like any perturbation theory in gravity, the EFT of magnetogenesis requires the inclusion of fluctuations in the matter and metric degrees of freedom. As mentioned above, in the EFT of inflation, one makes a specific gauge choice in which the inflaton fluctuations vanish
[
155
]
. To consistently investigate magnetogenesis, we need to include the vector perturbations in the metric
[
44
,
296
,
199
]
. In this appendix, we show that the vector perturbations do not have temporal evolution; hence, only the gauge field is the relevant gauge-invariant variable for the EFT.

We consider vector perturbations about a spatially flat
(3+1)-
dimensional FRW line element:

ds^{2}=\overline{g}_{\mu\nu}dx^{\mu}dx^{\nu}=N^{2}(t)dt^{2}-a^{2}(t)\delta_{ij}dx^{i}dx^{j}=N^{2}(\eta)\,d\eta^{2}-a(\eta)^{2}\delta_{ij}dx^{i}dx^{j}\,.

(D.1)

The only degree of freedom in the metric is the usual expansion parameter
a(t)
, a function only of time by virtue of the homogeneity of the three-space. The lapse function
N(t)
simply represents the time reparametrization invariance.

The conformal time (
\eta
),
is related to the cosmic time by the relation
t=\int d\eta a(\eta)
,
a(\eta)
is the scale factor and
N(\eta)=a(\eta)
.
H
is the
Hubble parameter given by
H\equiv\dot{a}/{a}
while
\mathcal{H}\equiv a^{\prime}/a
is related to the Hubble parameter by the relation
\mathcal{H}=H\,a
.

At the linear level, for a single scalar field inflation with gauge-field
(A_{\mu})
, the metric perturbations (
\delta g_{\mu\nu}
) can be categorized into three distinct types — scalar, vector and tensor perturbations
[
297
,
40
]
. As mentioned above, we will consider the gauge field perturbations for magnetogenesis. Hence, the perturbed FRW line-element corresponding to vector perturbations can be written as
[
297
,
40
,
44
]
,

ds^{2}=a^{2}(\eta)\left[d\eta^{2}+2S_{i}d\eta dx^{i}-\left(\delta_{ij}+2F_{(i,j)}\right)\,dx^{i}dx^{j}\right]\,,

(D.2)

where
S_{i}
and
F_{i}
are pure vector contributions and are divergenceless
[
297
,
40
]
:

\partial_{i}S^{i}=0;~~\partial_{i}F^{i}=0;~~F_{(i,j)}=\frac{F_{i,j}+F_{j,i}}{2}\,.

(D.3)

The above-perturbed metric has four unknown vector-mode functions. Since there are only two physically relevant vector modes, we must make certain choices on
S_{i}
and
F_{i}
. We choose
[
44
]
:

F_{i}=0.

(D.4)

To keep calculations transparent, let us consider the following action
[
205
]
:

\mathcal{S}=S_{\rm EH}+S_{\rm Inflaton}+S_{\rm EM}+S_{\rm EM-NM}

(D.5)

where,

\displaystyle S_{\rm EH}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int d^{4}x\sqrt{-g}\frac{M_{\rm Pl}^{2}}{2}R\,,

\displaystyle S_{\rm Inflaton}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{4}x\sqrt{-g}\left[\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\mu}\phi\partial^{\mu}\phi-V(\phi)\right],

\displaystyle S_{\rm EM}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}\int d^{4}x\sqrt{-g}\,F_{\mu\nu}F^{\mu\nu}\,,

(D.6)

\displaystyle S_{\rm EM-NM}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{M^{2}}\int d^{4}x\sqrt{-g}\left[\Lambda_{1}(\varphi)RF_{\mu\nu}F^{\mu\nu}+\Lambda_{2}(\varphi)R^{\nu}_{\mu}F_{\nu\alpha}F^{\mu\alpha}+\Lambda_{3}(\varphi)R_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}F^{\mu\nu}F^{\alpha\beta}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\Lambda_{4}(\varphi)C_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}F^{\mu\nu}F^{\alpha\beta}+\Lambda_{5}(\varphi)\Box\varphi F_{\mu\nu}F^{\mu\nu}+\Lambda_{6}(\varphi)\partial_{\mu}\varphi\partial^{\nu}\varphi F^{\mu\alpha}F_{\nu\alpha}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\Lambda_{7}(\varphi)\nabla_{\mu}\nabla^{\nu}\varphi F_{\nu\alpha}F^{\mu\alpha}\right]

\phi
is the canonical scalar (inflaton) field,
V(\phi)
is the self-interacting potential,
\varphi=\phi/M
with
M
being some characteristic scale,

F_{\mu\nu}\equiv\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}-\partial_{\nu}A_{\mu}
is the electromagnetic tensor. Eq. (
D.6
) is the standard gauge-field action while action (
D.1
) corresponds to all possible non-minimal coupling terms of the gauge field with gravity and the inflaton
[
205
]
. Owing to the fact that the background space-time is FRW, the natural choice for the
classical
gauge field in the background space-time is
\bar{A}_{\mu}=0
